Their theorem gives exact BSD over $M$, and isogeny invariance transfers it to $E_M$.

Finally restriction of scalars is compatible with the BSD arithmetic volume, and $$\operatorname{Res}_{M/\mathbb Q}E_M\ \sim_{\mathbb Q}\ E\times E^M
 \ \sim_{\mathbb Q}\ E^2.$$ Thus the normalized BSD discrepancy for $E$ has square one. It is positive: analytic rank zero gives rank zero and finite $\mathop{\mathrm{Sha}}$, so the two-primary result supplies positivity and rationality. It is therefore one. This is the descent argument of Burungale–Flach, Corollary 2, using the restriction-of-scalars and isogeny compatibilities of <a id="pm-cite-106"></a>[[Cassels](#ref-Cassels), [MilneBSD](#ref-MilneBSD)]; the preceding lattice argument supplies the additional nonmaximal-order case. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-center-odd-valuations"></a>
<div id="center:odd-valuations" class="proposition">

**Proposition 74**. *For every elliptic curve $E/\mathbb Q$ of analytic rank zero or one and every odd prime $p$, $$v_p(Q_E)=v_p(\#\mathop{\mathrm{Sha}}(E/\mathbb Q)).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-center-odd-valuations"><summary>Proof of stmt-center-odd-valuations</summary>

*Proof.* For a non-CM curve, the single-curve inequality <a href="#eq:source-8" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-8">[eq:source-8]</a> gives $X(E)\geq0$. Its twist $E^D$ is also non-CM and has the complementary analytic rank, so the same inequality gives $X(E^D)\geq0$. Their sum is zero by <a href="#eq:source-24" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-24">[eq:source-24]</a>; hence $X(E)=0$.

For a CM curve of analytic rank zero use Lemma <a href="#center:cm-zero" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="center:cm-zero">73</a>. For a CM curve of analytic rank one, the companion $E^D$ is CM of analytic rank zero. That lemma and <a href="#eq:source-24" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-24">[eq:source-24]</a> again give $X(E)=0$. The construction was made for an arbitrary odd $p$, including $p=3$, so the displayed equality holds at every odd prime. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-center-odd-valuations-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-center-odd-valuations</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.* Apply the low-corank converse (Theorem <a href="#classical:low-corank" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="classical:low-corank">2</a>) at the prime $q$ in the hypothesis. It gives $a(E)=r(E)=s_q(E)\in\{0,1\}$ and finiteness of the whole $\mathop{\mathrm{Sha}}(E/\mathbb Q)$. The Kummer exact sequence then gives $s_p(E)=r(E)$ for every prime $p$, in particular $s_2(E)\leq1$. The two-primary formula (Theorem <a href="#classical:two-primary" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="classical:two-primary">3</a>) gives positivity and rationality of $Q_E$ and its correct $2$-adic valuation. Proposition <a href="#center:odd-valuations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="center:odd-valuations">74</a> supplies its correct valuation at every odd prime, independently of $q$. Consequently the positive rational number $Q_E/\#\mathop{\mathrm{Sha}}(E/\mathbb Q)$ has zero valuation at every rational prime. Its prime factorization is empty, so it equals one. Substituting the definition of $Q_E$ is exactly the asserted real formula, with the total real period and full Mordell–Weil regulator. ◻

</details>

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "The pair comparison separates integral divisibility from divisor removal. The theta implication is used on characteristic-zero divisors; the final specialization takes place in the integral ring.",
      "figure_id": "fig:pair-comparison",
      "pdf_page": 6,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:pair-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "The pair comparison separates integral divisibility from\ndivisor removal. The theta implication is used on characteristic-zero\ndivisors; the final specialization takes place in the integral ring.",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n box/.style={draw,align=center,text width=5.5cm,inner sep=6pt},\n >=Stealth,node distance=9mm]\n \\node[box] (integral) {Integral character division\\\\\n                         $U_w,U_{\\bar w}\\in R$, $p\\nmid U_wU_{\\bar w}$};\n \\node[box,below=of integral] (theta) {Theta regularity and character division\\\\\n                         $(U_w)=(U_{\\bar w})$};\n \\node[box,below=of theta] (remove) {New tame directions exclude\\\\\n                         simultaneous jumps in codimension one};\n \\node[box,below=of remove] (unit) {$U_w,U_{\\bar w}\\in R^\\times$\\\\\n                         Exact integral specialization at the center};\n \\draw[->] (integral)--(theta);\n \\draw[->] (theta)--(remove);\n \\draw[->] (remove)--(unit);\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A vertical flowchart consisting of four rectangular boxes connected sequentially by three centered downward-pointing arrows:\n- First box (top): contains the text \"Integral character division\" on the first line, and \"$U_w, U_{\\bar{w}} \\in R, p \\nmid U_w U_{\\bar{w}}$\" on the second line.\n- A vertical downward arrow connects the first box to the second box.\n- Second box: contains the text \"Theta regularity and\" on the first line, \"character division\" on the second line, and \"$(U_w) = (U_{\\bar{w}})$\" on the third line.\n- A vertical downward arrow connects the second box to the third box.\n- Third box: contains the text \"New tame directions exclude\" on the first line, \"simultaneous jumps\" on the second line, and \"in codimension one\" on the third line.\n- A vertical downward arrow connects the third box to the fourth box.\n- Fourth box (bottom): contains \"$U_w, U_{\\bar{w}} \\in R^\\times$\" on the first line, and \"Exact integral specialization at the center\" on the subsequent lines (with \"specialization\" broken as \"special-ization\")."
    }

      "basis": "stated",
      "direction": "directed",
      "explanation": "The low-corank converse is imported from the companion converse paper.",
      "relation": "uses",
      "source_anchor": "stmt-classical-low-corank",
      "source_pm_id": "PM-002.1",
      "source_quote": "For every elliptic curve $A/\\mathbb Q$, every prime $q$, and $u\\in\\{0,1\\}$, the equality $s_q(A)=u$ implies $$a(A)=r(A)=u,\\qquad \\#\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sha}}(A/\\mathbb Q)<\\infty.$$",
      "target_anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
      "target_pm_id": "PM-002.2",
      "target_quote": "There is no restriction on reduction type, complex multiplication, the residual representation, rational torsion, or rational isogenies.",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    },
    {
      "basis": "stated",
      "direction": "directed",
      "explanation": "The two-primary valuation is imported from the companion formula paper.",
      "relation": "uses",
      "source_anchor": "stmt-classical-two-primary",
      "source_pm_id": "PM-002.1",
      "source_quote": "For every elliptic curve $A/\\mathbb Q$ with $s_2(A)\\leq1$, the quotient $Q_A$ of <a href=\"#eq:normalized-quotient\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:normalized-quotient\">[eq:normalized-quotient]</a>, with the total real period and the full-lattice regulator of <a href=\"#eq:height-convention\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:height-convention\">[eq:height-convention]</a>, is a positive rational number, and $$v_2(Q_A)=v_2(\\#\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sha}}(A/\\mathbb Q)).$$",
      "target_anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
      "target_pm_id": "PM-002.3",
      "target_quote": "Let $E/\\mathbb Q$ be any elliptic curve with $s_2(E)\\leq1$.",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    },
    {
      "basis": "stated",
      "direction": "directed",
      "explanation": "The twist density theorem is used to choose auxiliary quadratic fields.",
      "relation": "uses",
      "source_anchor": "stmt-classical-twist-density",
      "source_pm_id": "PM-002.1",
      "source_quote": "For every elliptic curve $A/\\mathbb Q$, among nonzero squarefree integers $d$ ordered by $|d|$, the twists $A^d$ of analytic rank zero and one each have density $1/2$.",
      "target_anchor": "stmt-thm-main-density",
      "target_pm_id": "PM-006.1",
      "target_quote": "Consequently the twists of analytic rank at least two have density zero.",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    },
    {
      "basis": "inferred",
      "direction": "directed",
      "explanation": "The source paper proves the complete exact Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer formula at every prime, superseding the two-adic valuation equality obtained in the target paper.",
      "relation": "supersedes",
      "source_anchor": "thm:main",
      "source_pm_id": "PM-002.1",
      "source_quote": "There are no additional hypotheses on reduction, rational torsion, isogenies, complex multiplication, or residual Galois representations.",
      "target_anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
      "target_pm_id": "PM-002.3",
      "target_quote": "There is no restriction on $E[2]$ or on the reduction of $E$ at two.",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    }
  ],
  "generation_status": "COMPLETE",
  "graph_revision": "store-1493",
  "pm_id": "PM-002.1",
  "schema": "pm.edges.v2"
}
